# Foundation 4: latest-library integration and full-library audit plan

Date: 2026-11-12 (Asia/Dhaka). Status: **93/95 implementation criteria checked; exact-head PHP 8.4/8.5 analysis and functional QA pass; primary and confirmation P6 comparisons fail. Batch 168 fixed historical benchmark fixture compatibility; the completed exact-head CI has two genuine sustained-host comparison failures. Batch 169 isolates and optimizes candidate PHP-FPM per-request trusted-manifest staging; both P6 certifications require fresh verification.**


## Batch 169 — preserve trusted manifest validation without PHP-FPM request-time file creation (one commit; CI pending)

- **Evidence and release blocker:** [Security & Standards #43161002861](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/43161002861) completed **50 successful, 2 failed, 3 skipped**. All 24 native paired-host measurement jobs succeeded on actual immutable source SHAs; both **source-only comparison jobs** failed the unchanged **≤2% successful-RPM regression** and **≤2.5% CV** budgets. [Primary artifact 13920408066](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/43161002861/artifacts/13920408066) contains **5 passing, 3 regressions, 4 high-variance cases**; [confirmation artifact 13920965763](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/43161002861/artifacts/13920965763) contains **3 passing, 3 regressions, 6 high-variance cases**. FPM is the repeated regression: fpm/1/10 **−4.926%, −5.974%**; fpm/4/1 **−5.508%, −10.328%**. Runwire did not show a confirmed >2% regression in the primary or confirmation comparison.
- **Isolated candidate-specific FPM hot path:** `WebReleaseRuntime::loadTrustedManifest()` currently SHA-verifies an immutable PHP manifest, creates a cryptographically named directory, writes a fresh executable PHP file, executes it via Webrick's `ReleaseManifestLoader`, then deletes file and directory. PHP-FPM repeats this on **every request**, unlike Runwire worker startup. Local PHP 8.4 tests reproduced roughly **0.33–0.62 ms/call** for temporary-file staging, compared with **~0.01–0.02 ms/call** for historical direct-hash/file loading. The precise fraction of hosted RPM regression remains unproven; do not count this local microbenchmark as P6 certification.
- **Targeted optimization:** retain original SHA-256 verification on captured source bytes and Webrick's exact native `ReleaseManifestLoader` schema validation, but expose those verified bytes through a **short-lived read-only, per-request PHP stream** instead of materializing fresh executable files. The already-captured manifest snapshot cannot change between verification and load; stream contents are held only for the validation call, keyed by an unpredictable identity, and removed in `finally`. No `eval()`, request-time generated code/files, schema bypass or loosened trust boundary. Works for both native PHP runtime manifest and JSON fallback. Local PHP 8.4 stream smoke testing succeeded for both forms; a 1,000-iteration smoke microbenchmark measured **~0.009 ms/call**, not representative of hosted P6 throughput.
- **Tests:** add PHP/JSON native-loader parity and invalid-schema rejection tests; keep existing immutable-hash, release integrity and trust-race tests in place. This is a **hypothesis-driven source optimization requiring a fresh exact-commit run** to determine whether P6 FPM RPM and variance genuinely recover. Do not claim success before the completed GitHub matrix and all other QA jobs pass.
- **Unchanged contracts:** Foundation release semantics, actual baseline/source SHAs, full paired-host matrix, worker CPU affinity, throughput/variation budgets, Composer locks and upstream dependencies, PHPForge `dev-main@dev`, Runwire `^3.0.1`, draft/open PR, no merge, tag or release.

## Batch 168 — restore shared benchmark fixture for genuine historical-baseline checkout (one commit; new-head QA pending)

- **New failure after Batch 167:** [Security & Standards #43160090141](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/43160090141) reaches verified exact baseline/candidate SHA checkouts, then fails multiple source-only paired-host measurement jobs (first observed primary `fpm/1/1`, `fpm/1/10`, `runwire/4/1`) before meaningful throughput measurement: `Class "Infocyph\Foundation\Tests\Fixtures\ProbeInput" not found` at `baseline/benchmarks/Support/BenchmarkSupport.php:89`. Other paired matrix jobs were still running when triaged; do not assume their verdicts.
- **Root cause:** the existing host workflow installs candidate benchmark tooling into the genuinely historical immutable baseline `c220759fcbd6654fead843599712a5b79685f02d`. That baseline predates `tests/Fixtures/ProbeInput.php`, but modern `BenchmarkSupport::removeDirectory()` requires it. The earlier unpinned baseline checkout inadvertently hid this missing fixture, so the first exact-source run exposes it correctly. This is a **benchmark harness compatibility failure**, not established runtime/P6 performance regression.
- **Minimal fix:** in the shared tool preparation step, copy the candidate's development-only `tests/Fixtures/ProbeInput.php` into the baseline checkout alongside the existing benchmarks overlay; byte-compare the fixture, install the existing locked Composer graphs and verify both fresh autoloaders resolve the exact fixture class before starting matched-host trials. Do not modify either revision's `src/` runtime implementation or dependency graph, change measurement algorithms, alter CPU isolation or relax the P6 matrix.
- **Required qualification:** rerun exact-head QA and paired-host measurements after this commit. The preceding failed run is *not* P6 evidence and cannot be counted as the source-only certification. Both source-only and independently locked dependency-upgrade P6 gates remain open at **93/95, P0 25/25**; `composer.lock` ignored, PHPForge `dev-main@dev`, Runwire `^3.0.1`, PR draft/unmerged, no tag or release.

## Batch 167 — fail closed on the exact compared source SHAs (one commit; hosted QA pending)

- **Verified input:** PR source head `53881f45ddf3b8704fe2d24e27f047c2d2401b01`; [Security & Standards run 43148590307](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/43148590307) has **50 successful, 2 failed and 3 skipped jobs**. Both strict Psalm/PHPStan analysis jobs, all four Pest QA lanes, production consumers and ordinary benchmarks passed. Source-only primary and confirmation host-comparison jobs failed. No release certification is claimed.
- **Independent artifact analysis:** [primary comparison artifact 13915207385](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/43148590307/artifacts/13915207385) reports **10 pass, 2 inconclusive_variance** (FPM w1/c1 CV 3.509%; FPM w4/c1 CV 2.707%); [confirmation artifact 13915865622](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/43148590307/artifacts/13915865622) reports **4 pass, 8 inconclusive_variance** (CV up to 7.625%). These do not qualify under the original **≤2.5% CV** and **≤2% regression** rules, and do not establish a trustworthy candidate regression while source checkouts are unverified.
- **Source identity defect:** the PR-label `workflow_call` did not supply `baseline_ref`. The paired-host baseline checkout used that empty input, although measurements were labeled as immutable baseline `c220759fcbd6654fead843599712a5b79685f02d`. In addition, the preparation checkout used the PR default ref, recording synthetic merge SHA `86e04805e549b39001f5b0551513f888b826f9bc` as its candidate, not PR head `53881f45ddf3b8704fe2d24e27f047c2d2401b01`. The original paired comparison is **invalid as exact-source certification evidence**, regardless of its numeric result.
- **Fix:** select the exact PR head in the prepare checkout (or the triggering ref for manual dispatch), select the documented immutable baseline in both invocation modes, verify preparation source identity before resolving locks, and verify both actual source checkouts and distinct SHAs in each paired runner **before** dependency installs/measurements. Preserve the existing unchanged host runner, matrix, five 120-second samples, soak and 2%/2.5% budgets. No benchmark algorithm, acceptance threshold, library dependency, sanitizer policy or analyzer configuration changes.
- **Qualification still required:** exact-head Security & Standards and native-host verification after this commit, authentic source-only P6 primary/confirmation matrices with measured actual SHAs, separately constructed and reviewed independent-lock upgrade P6, and release packaging/consumer requalification. The earlier maintainer contract integration is now committed at `53881f45`; its local `13/13` result is superseded for strict analysis/QA by that SHA's successful hosted jobs, but neither P6 mode is accepted. `composer.lock` remains ignored, PHPForge remains `dev-main@dev`, Runwire remains `^3.0.1`. PR stays draft/open/unmerged; no tag or release.

## Current update integration — 2026-11-12 (local CI verified)

- **Scope and decision:** retain the maintainer's native type updates and adapt the owning interfaces, input boundaries and callers, as explicitly requested. `composer ic:process` was run successfully. The original ten-file patch is retained at `/tmp/foundation-review-20261112/user-start.patch`; the working tree is based on `9603a1ee`. No commit or publication was performed.
- **Baseline findings:** TTL `put()` and terminal `json()` had incompatible interface declarations, causing fatal autoload errors. Psalm reported **11 blocking errors**, including literal-only parameter contracts and repeated guards. Skipper rejected the changed sanitizer hash. These are resolved through contract integration rather than restoring the old mixed native signatures.
- **Integrated contracts:** `CommandIO::json(array)` now matches `TerminalIO`; structured payloads can contain nested scalars and objects. Both TTL implementations and their interface use array/scalar/null value and default unions. Boolean passwordless/MFA markers remain supported. JSON success and JSON-path assertion methods retain native unions, including objects so resources and serializable responses continue working. The monitor retains its array parameter. WebAuthn hosts and OAuth redirect helpers are native strings after validation. Session and flash values keep their intentional mixed storage boundary; sample-only annotations are replaced by maintained contract descriptions.
- **CLI boundary:** `ProbeInput::arguments(array|string|null)` retains the maintainer's strict signature. `fromServer(array)` validates raw server metadata before calling it, rejecting invalid boolean, numeric, object and resource values with the validation exception. All **15** affected fixture/benchmark CLI entrypoints now use this boundary. Three command-output fixtures retain assertions about emitted results while replacing repeated array guards with the native interface contract. Closed standard input remains safe.
- **Security:** path, source-project and loopback sanitizer algorithms remain unchanged; the new server-metadata boundary validates input before entering the typed argument contract. The same six exact, default-deny policy entries pin reviewed SHA256 `5469a5fe8820559bd624098850a9f283c512f1b6783c5232f60ec00342da8cd8` and the hostile-input regression suite. No escape category, suppression, exclusion, level or threshold was added or weakened.
- **Verified result:** final `composer ic:ci` exits **0**, with **13/13 PASS**, including the full **1,023-test** functional suite, PHPStan, Psalm, strict comments, skipper, syntax, references, duplicates, normalization, formatting, architecture and Rector. Both analyzers have zero blocking errors under the unchanged configurations. All **1,003 source/configuration input hashes** remain unchanged after this final run on PHP **8.5.4**. Actual `composer ic:process` runs passed, including a second reviewed run against the integrated native contracts. All **six AuthBench subjects** pass `composer ic:bench:quick`; this is functional smoke evidence, not throughput/P6 certification. Updated caller/API guidance is in [the migration guide](foundation-4-migration.md#typed-command-and-authentication-state-boundaries).
- **Dependencies and release boundary:** all **172 installed package versions and source/dist references** remain unchanged; only sanitizer policy and ignored lock metadata changed. Implementation remains **93/95, P0 25/25**. Both P6 certifications, exact-commit hosted QA, archives and production consumers remain separate release work. Dependency audit/toolchain maintenance evidence remains dated and was not refreshed by this source review.
- **Evidence and cleanup:** `/tmp/foundation-review-20261112/ci-accepted.log` records the final gate; `ci-accepted-inputs.json` pins its inputs. The same directory retains the original patch, complete baseline/follow-up diagnostics, standalone functional checkpoints, processor/benchmark logs and rule checklist. Earlier checkpoints remain historical evidence. Only task-owned Compose project `foundation_ci_20261112` was removed after verification: all five containers and its network; other projects were untouched. No commit, tag or publication was performed.

## Previous whole-codebase CI remediation — 2026-11-11 (local CI verified)

- **Authorized scope:** repair the entire current `composer ic:ci` scan at base `365425bc`. Public/runtime/security contracts, all analyzer rules/paths/levels, behavioral assertions and release budgets remain enforced. Earlier 50-file cohorts are historical evidence, not current gate certification.
- **Baseline:** `composer ic:ci` exited **2**, with **881 PHPStan errors**, **527 Psalm errors**, and **990 passed / 26 failed** functional tests. With isolated PHPForge MySQL/PostgreSQL/Redis/Valkey/Memcached fixtures, the unchanged functional baseline passed **1,016 tests / 37,247 assertions**. Required services were provisioned rather than waived or skipped.
- **Repairs:** restore broad public JSON input/assertion contracts and add mixed-scalar coverage; correct real service, nested metadata, native-store, optional-context, JSON/YAML/CLI and exception boundaries; retain native Runwire cancellation and scope ownership; give legitimate first-party internal tests their owning namespaces and preserve compiled named handlers; read generated fixture arrays through the existing captured-byte loader. Opaque scheduled observations use explicit mutable flags and accurate callback return contracts. Duplicate type predicates were replaced by equivalent runtime validation or stronger behavior/identity checks.
- **Security:** the load harness now reconstructs loopback URLs from an exact host, validated native port and finite endpoint set, rejecting user-info redirects, remote hosts, queries and fragments. `ProbeInput` confines data paths to canonical local workspaces and rejects wrappers, traversal and symlink escapes; source-project authority additionally rejects world-writable directories and shell controls. Four hostile-input/bootstrap regression tests cover these boundaries. Composer's PHPForge policy remains **default deny**, authorizing only six exact sanitizer categories on this one development-only file, pinned to reviewed SHA256 `4889f5cec4ffa08c01d13348c2c7c3029b8573af46b31a3dc218a601aac0c6f6` and separate regression evidence. Data paths retain include/shell taint. Configuration normalization preserves individual override keys so database data-path taint cannot be conflated with executable configuration roots; no production taint escape or suppression was added.
- **Benchmarks:** split evidence validation and sample aggregation into cohesive functions while retaining formulas, counts and certification thresholds. The AuthBench smoke additionally exposed missing request scopes for MFA/passkey setup and execution; those two subjects now exercise the required native request lifecycle and require fresh timing baselines. All **six AuthBench subjects**, **four representative workloads** and both isolated Phase 9 HTTP variants pass functional smoke checks. These smoke runs do not certify performance or P6.
- **Verified result:** final `composer ic:ci` exits **0** with **13/13 PASS**: skipper, Composer normalization, syntax, references, duplicates, comment policy, Pest, Pint, PHPCS, Deptrac, PHPStan, Psalm and Rector. Both analyzers pass with **zero blocking errors** under the unchanged configurations. The full functional suite contains **1,021 passing tests**; the latest standalone functional run recorded **38,955 assertions**. All six AuthBench subjects, four representative workloads and both Phase 9 HTTP variants also pass smoke checks. No source/configuration input changed during the final CI run: **1,002 input hashes** were checked afterward. This certifies the uncommitted local working tree based on `365425bc` on PHP **8.5.4**, with isolated PHPForge service fixtures; it is not a hosted-commit or release certification.
- **Evidence and cleanup:** final gate output is `/tmp/foundation-ci-20261111/ci-final-2.log`; source/configuration hashes are in `ci-inputs-before.json`; detailed analyzer inventories, smoke logs, audit JSON and the completed rule checklist are in the same directory. Temporary services belonged only to Compose project `foundation_ci_20261111` and were removed after verification. MySQL/PostgreSQL/Redis/Valkey/Memcached and their exported fixture environment remain prerequisites for reproducing the full integration run. No skipped tests, weaker rules, lower levels, exclusions, baselines or vendor edits were introduced.
- **Dependencies:** Composer lock metadata was refreshed for the extra policy; all **172 package versions and source/dist references remain unchanged**, and standalone `composer audit --locked --format=json` reports **zero advisories** but exits **1** for abandonment. The existing abandoned `doctrine/annotations` package is required by development-only `phpbench/phpbench 1.7.0`; replacing that upstream requirement is maintenance work, not a failed `ic:ci` task. Existing Pest/PHPUnit and Psalm/JsonMapper version ceilings remain visible in the dependency tracker. Runwire remains `^3.0.1`, PHPForge remains `dev-main@dev`, and the lock remains gitignored.
- **Release boundary:** **93/95 implementation criteria, P0 25/25** remain unchanged. Exact-commit hosted PHP 8.4/8.5 checks, packaging/consumer verification and both P6 sustained certifications remain open. Changes remain uncommitted; no merge, tag or publication is claimed.

## Batch 159b — nullable-string TTL contract repair (P0 CI regression, exact-head QA pending)

- **Cause:** commit `b8083fd8d665f48c2ebeaacca96ed6e667feb7bb` narrowed `CacheLayerTtlStore::pull()` to `?string` while `TtlStoreInterface` still declared `mixed`. PHP method incompatibility causes fatal errors in Pest, benchmarks and source-only paired-host setup, independently of throughput. PHPStan also aborts one analysis subprocess; strict Psalm reports 739 findings per PHP 8.4/8.5 at the broken head, versus 724 at Batch 159a.
- **Owner decision:** `pull(string $key, ?string $default = null): mixed` is the contract. Change both the interface and its two implementations (`CacheLayerTtlStore`, `ArrayTtlStore`), and relax the over-specific Psalm sentinel annotation to allow any nullable string. Add a real integration regression for both backends: missing key, custom string sentinel, and consuming stored array values only once. `get()` and `put()` retain their mixed value contracts.
- **Exception to batch sizing:** This is a targeted high-priority compatibility repair rather than a 50–100-file static-analysis cohort; keep it in one commit and do not represent it as a complete batch. No dependency changes, performance budget changes, suppressions, exclusions, baseline changes, lock-file policy changes, merge, tag or release.
- **Acceptance:** Exact-commit PHP 8.4/8.5 QA and analyses, benchmarks, production consumers, native hosts and paired-host setup must be rechecked. Correcting fatal declaration incompatibility does not qualify either outstanding P6 sustained comparison.

## Current implementation and maintenance status

The [consolidated review](foundation-4-consolidated-review.md#current-maintenance-and-tracker-synchronization) owns the current findings and verification evidence. Earlier batch entries below are historical snapshots; their old counts, unresolved findings and package/CI claims do not describe the current working tree.

- **Implementation checklist: 93/95 complete; P0 25/25.** The three repaired R01/R04 criteria are checked for completed source work and local regression evidence. Only the two P6 release certifications remain unchecked. Final exact-revision hosted QA, service-backed checks, production-consumer/archive qualification and host certification remain separate release verification; no new hosted result or release readiness is claimed.
- **R01–R07 source findings:** repairs are retained. Compound PIN redaction and same-second/prior-consent zero-age OIDC reuse are covered by the expanded in-memory, SQLite and real GET/valid-CSRF POST regressions. R01/R04 repair evidence remains under `/tmp/foundation-fix-residuals/`.
- **I01 / M01 implemented:** passwordless login links now require one successful atomic redemption. Both token drivers persist an expiring hashed marker before notification and verify cryptography before native TTL consumption. The public manager, immutable graph recipe, TTL contract, authentication/migration docs and release notes agree. Existing stateless links must be reissued; shared persistent atomic auth state is required across processes. Replay, expiry, missing state, storage failure and independent-process contention regressions are tracked in `PasswordlessSingleUseTest`.
- **I02 / M04 review complete:** the unchanged PHPForge duplicate gate passes. The original 131-group observation is historical. Inspected current key-resolution/validation clones retain distinct defaults, key purposes, diagnostics and audit/validation ownership; timed-token common mechanics already live in the two native representation-specific abstract services. Retain this bounded duplication rather than introduce another generic protocol owner. No detector budget or suppression changes, and no claim that all token-similar code was removed.
- **M02/M03 upstream blockers verified:** live Composer resolution finds no compatible updates. PHPBench 1.7.0 still requires abandoned `doctrine/annotations` 2.0.2; Pest 5.3.5 conflicts with PHPUnit above 13.4.5; Psalm 7.0.5 and RPC 3.2.3 require JsonMapper 5 while 6 is latest. These development-only upstream items remain externally blocked, not fixed or waived. See the [dependency disposition](foundation-4-dependency-qualification.md#current-maintenance-disposition).
- **Final local checks:** required PHPForge process and all static/quality/architecture gates pass, including PHPStan and Psalm. Focused regressions: **44 passed / 5,575 assertions**. Final full local suite: **990 passed, 25 unavailable-service failures / 36,937 assertions**, with the same 25 failed identities as the previous baseline. Strict Composer validation and local documentation links pass. Evidence: `/tmp/foundation-maintenance/qa-final.log`, `focused-final.log` and `validation-summary.json`.
- **Source checkpoint:** maintenance changes are committed at `d30abb10b11ab61d080e064cd34e213a838ec0f7`. Add the real `SessionPrincipalResolver` and `SessionManager` rotation test to prove persisted `recentAuthAt` survives rotation without becoming the new session's creation timestamp, including unknown auth time.
- **R04 CSRF-positive controller boundary:** Exercise the real `OAuthAuthorizationController` through native `CsrfMiddleware` with a genuine generated browser CSRF token and a published Epicrypt OIDC client: stale/missing/fresh evidence, silent requests, `prompt=login`, `select_account` and `max_age=0`. Assert denials carry OAuth errors, never issue a code nor grant consent. Require a genuinely fresh positive consent POST to issue a code. Also verify forged CSRF returns 419. Full router route registration is already tested separately by `OAuth21FoundationHttpIntegrationTest`; do not mislabel a direct controller + real middleware test as full web host dispatch.
- **R07 failure ordering:** Add real inline history-storage failure after Pending/Running records succeeded, ensuring secondary failure recording does not override the authoritative command exception. Existing supervised lock tests cover primary+release failure and otherwise-successful release failure.
- **Acceptance:** These are independent reviewed regression additions; keep all nine review checkboxes unchecked until this exact-head run and the official native hosts are green. Formal **84/95**, P6 unchanged; PR draft/unmerged, no tag/release.

## Foundation 4 Batch 125 — retain original borrowed Runwire owner during lazy response emission

- **R05:** Pathwise's initial download preparation already checks the borrowed request, but the Webrick response generator is advanced later outside that binding; cancellation after preparation or between chunks previously read onward. This is a native HTTP streaming lifecycle defect, not a reason to change Pathwise's adapter or own the host event loop.
- **Fix:** Capture original `CacheRunwire::current()` when creating the response and bind its original `RunwireExecutionContext` only around each `DownloadProcessor::streamChunks()` iterator advance; restore context before yielding the chunk to Webrick. Do not reacquire the current emitter request, retain a completed owner, or change local file/HEAD/range/offload responses.
- **Regression:** A genuine nonlocal `uploads://` response under borrowed Runwire request is canceled *after preparation before first read* and *between two chunk reads*; each actual producer advance must throw native cancellation. Existing synchronous streaming and native host smoke remain required. R05/P3 remain unchecked until independent Fiber/abandonment coverage and fully green official CI.
- **Acceptance:** Formal **84/95**, P6 performance deliberately deferred, no merge/tag/release.

- Declare the actual OIDC protocol and authentication/container exceptions for the new owner methods, and separate the cache helper's state assignment from `try` as the official PER/Pint rule requires. Do not suppress Psalm, change authentication behavior, or widen scope.
- Require full current-head PHP 8.4/8.5 QA, production-consumer and native hosts before accepting new review items. **84/95** remains accurate. P6 performance deliberately deferred by maintainer.

## Foundation 4 Batch 124 — require actual OIDC authentication proof on GET, POST and code issuance

- **R03/R04:** The original OIDC interaction forged fresh `auth_time` for principals missing actual session login evidence; direct code issuance likewise substituted the issuance clock. HTTP POST `decision=approve` skipped the GET interaction policy. These were independently reproduced and are P1 release blockers.
- **Fix:** Propagate authoritative `AuthSession::recentAuthAt` through the session principal. Unknown, zero, invalid or future OIDC auth time is unauthenticated in Epicrypt, and direct OIDC code issue rejects unverified time. POST approval now evaluates the same Epicrypt interaction policy as GET before consent mutation or code issuance and refuses required login/account selection and `prompt=none` failures.
- **Regression:** Native OpenID fixed-clock regression covers missing, invalid, future, expired and valid fresh authentication; existing authorization HTTP and ID-token tests must remain green. More real-route CSRF/reauth/ACR tests remain required before accepting R04.
- **Acceptance:** Keep A08 R03 and R04 unchecked until full exact-head official quality and real HTTP regressions pass. Formal **84/95**, no merge or tag.

## Foundation 4 Batch 123 — preserve primary command failures through history and lock cleanup

- **R07:** `CommandExecutionCoordinator::run()` previously let a lock release error replace the primary command exception; failed-history recording can also throw while reporting an earlier failure. `RuntimeControl::mutate()` had the same unguarded lock cleanup order. Reuse existing `Runtime\\CleanupGuard` instead of adding an exception framework.
- **Fix:** Track original command/runtime-control exception. Attempt failed-history recording and lock release without replacing the primary, but propagate cleanup failure if work otherwise succeeds. Release acquired locks exactly once, without releasing a failed overlap acquisition. Keep inline exceptions authoritative when history storage is unavailable.
- **Regression:** Public coordinator tests with actual fake `LockProviderInterface` verify primary `LogicException` survives a failing release, successful work surfaces release failure, and overlap rejection never releases a nonexistent lock. Retain existing CleanupGuard and supervised process suite.
- **Acceptance:** R07 remains unchecked until exact-head official QA. Formal **84/95**, PR draft/unmerged; no tag, release or performance waiver.

## Foundation 4 Batch 122 — protect environment secret staging at first write

- **R06:** A private final `.env` is insufficient if its exclusively created temporary sibling receives plaintext under the process umask `0022` before `chmod 0600`. Original review demonstrates an actual writable `0644` stage at the first write, not a successful external-user exploit.
- **Fix:** After exclusive `fopen(..., 'x')`, set private mode and validate `fstat` **before** any bytes are written. Preserve existing full-write/flush/atomic-rename/cleanup behavior; do not change process-wide umask.
- **Regression:** Exercise actual public environment-secret generation under permissive umask, observe the temporary stream's permissions immediately before the first `fwrite` via a test-only namespaced fixture, and fault-inject denied `chmod` to ensure no write, no stage residue and original file preserved. Test helpers remain outside production source.
- **Acceptance:** Remains unchecked until full exact-head official CI. Formal **84/95**, no merge/tag/release or performance waiver.

## Foundation 4 Batch 121 — prevent provenance promotion at authorization, delegation and failed login audit sinks

- **R02 review:** `AuditingAuthorizer::can()`, `DelegationManager::recordAudit()` and `Authenticator::failure()` explicitly assign free-form caller context `session_id`/`device_id` to canonical audit fields, contradicting the accepted security boundary. The two managers and failed-login branch lack evidence that these IDs come from an authoritative session/record.
- **Repair:** Stop promoting those values. Preserve metadata through the existing recorder sanitizer and continue passing real trusted record/session identities explicitly in already-qualified successful login, token and device flows. Do not infer session ownership from an arbitrary principal metadata bag.
- **Regression:** Exercise public actual authorizer deny, delegated grant/revoke and invalid login with forged session/device IDs and assert only descriptive metadata retains them; verify DBLayer/SQLite persisted canonical columns are null for these paths. Separately prove explicit trusted session/device IDs persist when intentionally supplied by a trusted caller. Keep the default negative behavior stable for all other flows.
- **Acceptance:** Keep R02 and A04 audit checkbox unchecked until the completed official cross-major QA/native host response is green. Batch 120's R01 matrix and no-inflight-generation rule remain intact. Formal **84/95** pending evidence; no merge, tag or performance waiver.

## Foundation 4 Batch 120a — respect DBLayer parameter safety in credential-matrix persistence test

- **Official exact-head finding:** [Security & Standards 42355500457](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/42355500457) Batch 120 added a complete synthetic audit credential matrix; the DBLayer/SQLite test sent its entire matrix as one `metadata` bound parameter of 3,512 bytes, violating the published DBLayer limit of **1,024 bytes**. PHPForge Pest failed in the new test on 8.4/8.5; PHPCS/Pint/static checks and the in-memory matrix passed. Do not change production DBLayer security limits to accommodate tests.
- **Fix:** Preserve every matrix key, spelling and sentinel assertion but split the persisted test into 12-key chunks, one bounded audit row per chunk per login/reset/MFA/passkey purpose. Verify expected row count, safe IDs, stored redactions and absence of original sensitive bytes in every genuine persisted JSON row.
- **Acceptance:** R01/R02 and A04 remain open until all official QA jobs and native hosts pass exact HEAD. Formal **84/95** unchanged, no custom workers/quality-rule waivers, PR draft/unmerged.

## Foundation 4 Batch 106d — fix retained passkey migration's multi-engine and migration-ledger contracts

- **Source of failure:** Before correction, the latest affected GitHub QA fixture reproduced the original Foundation 3 `credential_id VARCHAR(1024) UNIQUE` in all backends. MySQL UTF8MB4 rejects that legacy index because its maximum 3072-byte key permits at most 768 UTF8MB4 characters; the error was from the test's historical schema creation, not from a leaked credential hash. This is genuine information about original Foundation 3 MySQL deployment portability; do not claim the 1024-character index was installable in this environment.
- **Accurate compatibility fixture:** Keep the exact 1024-character legacy unique column for SQLite and PostgreSQL; for MySQL, use a realistic deployed VARCHAR(768) unique raw credential column, with two independent historical IDs and native Foundation 4 hash backfill. Retain the explicit documented difference from the original schema.
- **Other QA corrections:** `OAuth21SchemaUpgradeTest` now expects the newly registered passkey hash migration in the normal native DBLayer migration ledger. Order migration methods down/id/up according to the project's Pint rules; use explicit `unset($schema)` in the non-destructive down callback so PHPCS knows the preserved interface parameter is intentionally unused. No quality suppressions or SQL error bypass.
- **Formal acceptance remains 85/90; release gate P4 stays open** until source/dependency graph, actual retained migration, current schema equivalence and rollback are all certified. Both exact-head official workflows must pass with unchanged PHPForge and performance policies. No merge/tag/release.

## Foundation 4 Batch 106 — explicit Foundation 3 passkey identity upgrade migration (exact-head QA pending)

- **Newly confirmed retained-data break:** Original Foundation 3 source `fc9b3258` stored `auth_passkey_credentials.credential_id` as an opaque unique raw VARCHAR(1024), **without** `credential_id_hash`. Foundation 4 `DBLayerPasskeyCredentialStore` exclusively searches `credential_id_hash` and `AuthSchemaInstaller::readiness()` already requires it, but the previous installer migration set **did not create or backfill the column** on retained Foundation 3 tables. New installations already create it. Marking P4 data migration complete earlier would have lost accessibility to existing passkeys.
- **Migration implementation:** New small native DBLayer `AuthPasskeyIdentityHashSchema` runs after established auth/passkey revisions and is idempotent across a partially created nullable digest column. It adds a unique SHA3-256 string column only where missing, processes historical passkeys in bounded 200-record keyset batches, checks each raw opaque ID and existing digest, fills missing hashes with CAS-guarded SQL writes, checks native migration cancellation, and fails closed on corruption/concurrent mutation. A down migration never deletes the raw passkey ID or backfilled hash, allowing a coherent old-source rollback. Require passkey writes to be quiesced during the schema upgrade.
- **Real application coverage:** `Foundation4PersistedAuthContentionTest` now recreates the actual Foundation 3 passkey table, stores two distinct historical opaque credentials, calls the regular `AuthSchemaInstaller` through selected SQLite/MySQL/PostgreSQL real connections and verifies `DBLayerPasskeyCredentialStore::findByCredentialId` after backfill and again after an idempotent installer rerun. No synthetic database adapter or unchecked raw-ID rewriting.
- **Remaining gate:** This repairs a **demonstrated persisted-format migration gap**, but existing legacy `credential_id` unique VARCHAR index remains until separately verified against current unrestricted TEXT and cross-engine index changes. The P4 full authentic old-source/dependency-artifact rollback and P6 exact sustained paired-host matrices remain open. The original historical 3.x lock still cannot be fabricated. Formal 85/90; draft PR unmerged, no tag.

## Implementation checkpoint — 2026-11-05

- **Batch 28 real selected MySQL/PostgreSQL Omnibus durable ownership (2026-11-05; verified at `db948bb`):** Extended `OmnibusDurableIntegrationTest` with isolated per-run native Omnibus schema on the PHPForge-provisioned MySQL and PostgreSQL engines (no shared-table pollution). Against actual Foundation bus/consumer/DBLayer transport, test dispatch→reserve A→expire token without sleeping→reclaim B, reject both stale A mutations, acknowledge B, and then retry one genuinely failing handler attempt through the retained consumer with once-only B settlement, no orphan message or failure-store row. Engine credentials come from existing official `IC_MYSQL_*`/`IC_POSTGRES_*` CI services; no custom workers or database emulation. Source `83ef022`; the exact combined source `db948bb` passed all **four** official PHP 8.4/8.5 stable/lowest PHPForge functional QA lanes, four production consumers, clean install, both benchmarks and both PHPStan analyses / [Security & Standards 42012714022](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/42012714022), and all native HTTP/Swoole/OpenSwoole smoke jobs / [42012713448](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/42012713448). Required strict Psalm still fails, now **1,417 errors on each PHP version (five fewer than 1,422)**. This verifies the selected real-engine tests, **not** all Omnibus outbox/deadline/worker/production soak criteria. Formal tracker remains **53/90, 37 open**.

A native borrowed Runwire host cancels before reservation under **both** `HOST_CANCELLED` and `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`; worker must propagate `CancelledException`, retain queued message without failure-store mutation, release host context, then run that *same worker* normally for once-only delivery. This is a deterministic cancellation-reason test, **not** a real-clock deadline/production pool soak. In affected source, documented the two actual PSR Container exceptions for `MessagingRuntimeResolver::callable()` and `::service()` (four prior official Psalm `MissingThrowsDocblock` records) and removed one redundant `WorkerManager` Psalm annotation; Official [PHPForge 42012714022](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/42012714022) verifies the five direct findings disappeared without new propagation: strict Psalm **1,422 → 1,417 on each PHP version**, PHPStan green, all four functional lanes green; native host [42012713448](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/42012713448) passed. ## Historical implementation checkpoints — 2026-11-04

- **P3 UID 6.0.1 real Foundation generator regression (2026-11-05; exact-head QA pending):** Add an integration test using the actual `UidAuthIdGenerator`, `ExecutionId::generate()`, `Id::uuid2()` and worker execution with host-owned Runwire request/runtime seeds. Assert UUIDv7/ULID canonical outputs, config-selected overrides, rejection of unsupported UUIDv2 defaults, and uniqueness across eight interleaved Fibers (256 generated IDs). Native `Id::uuid7()` / `Id::ulid()` accept no generation-context argument and remain direct fast paths; do not invent explicit Runwire parameters. UUIDv2 remains available through native UID without exposing a redundant Foundation facade. This is behavioral evidence, not a claim of cross-process clock monotonicity or a release-ready host soak. UID P3 items remain unchecked until exact-head official QA; **39/90 accepted, 51 open**.

- **Focused strict Psalm propagation batch (2026-11-05; exact-head QA pending):** Official PHPForge [analysis 127053514334](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41969133056) reports 1,345 Psalm errors, mostly propagation/documentation, alongside successful PHPStan. Resolve **73 verified `MissingThrowsDocblock` findings** directly from that official log across `BrowserSession` (27), base `DBLayerStore` (19), `DBLayerPermissionStore` (12) and `FilesystemResponseFactory` (15), annotating the actual method-level propagated exception types. This is contract documentation, not a runtime behavior change. Leave other taint, dynamic-call and exception diagnostics for separate implementation batches; no suppressions, custom workers or analysis configuration relaxation. Keep all A01/P3 acceptance counts tied to official exact-head functional QA; formal **39/90, 51 open** pending final verification.

- **P3 ArrayKit official PHPForge correction (2026-11-05; QA pending):** [Security & Standards 41968521254](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41968521254) on `c4e4f69f` passed Pest, PHPCS, Deptrac and Rector but Pint identified one `ordered_class_elements` violation in `ConfigRepository`; PHPStan found downstream failures because expanding `ConfigRepository::all()` from `array<string,mixed>` to `array<array-key,mixed>` broke its published project-level string-namespace contract. Revert just that public-return widening: retain numeric namespaces in the internal lazy owner for `get(0)`/`get('0.flags.0')`, snapshot/restore, and exported-cache persistence while keeping `all()` string-key-only, as before. Correct the `lazyNamespaces` map's key PHPDoc to `array<array-key,true>` for PHP's numeric-string key conversion (resolves the new Psalm redundant-cast finding), reorder the new `changed()` method per Pint, and update the regression to assert public `all()` excludes numeric keys. No analyzer suppression or policy change. **Formal acceptance remains 39/90, P0 19/20, P3 ArrayKit open until exact-head QA.**

- **P0 A01 existing-address destination contract accepted; full notification matrix pending (2026-11-05):** Four official PHPForge functional QA configurations passed on `d16d44aa` / [41967893771](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41967893771), including direct outbound and post-issuance address-change protections; strict Psalm remains red at **1,345 errors**. Accept only A01's destination-purpose criterion: current-account verification is now enforced at HTTP, manager, conditional update and notifier boundaries; no arbitrary address-change flow is supported. Add a data-driven real TalkingBytes fake-Emailer regression covering all 13 `AuthNotificationType` values with top-level and nested attacker email/recipient overrides, correct account and purpose metadata, and missing/invalid recipient rejection. The final A01 notification-purpose criterion remains open until the new test passes. **Formal acceptance rises from 38/90 to 39/90, 51 open (P0 19/20); P3 ArrayKit remains pending exact-head QA.**

- **P3 ArrayKit 5.3.2 lazy configuration correctness (2026-11-05; official QA pending):** `ConfigRepository` delegates string reads to `LayeredLazyFileConfig` but its inherited `set()`/`fill()`/`forget()`/`merge()` mutations and `snapshot()`/`changed()`/`exportCache()` used an as-yet empty `Config::$items` while the lazy owner held real values. The repository also skipped integer namespace keys from `all()`, resolved integer keys without loading their lazy namespace, and reported numeric namespace IDs as integers despite a `list<string>` contract. Preserve read laziness; materialize the selected array once before mutation through the common inherited `assertWritable()` boundary, before snapshot/change/export, and when `get(null)` or `all()` requires the complete configuration. Resolve integer lookup through the lazy owner's string namespace, keep the existing string-key-only public `all()` contract while supporting numeric namespaces through `get(0)` and dot-path lookups, normalize `lazyNamespaces()` to strings. Add a current-ArrayKit integration regression covering `0.php` lazy namespace, snapshot isolation, dot-path changes, fill/forget/merge, external PHP array references, restore and exported cache reload. **P3 ArrayKit acceptance remains unchecked until official exact-head QA; overall 38/90.**

- **P0 A01 username/address parser parity and affected Psalm (2026-11-05; QA pending):** Published TalkingBytes 2.3.1 `EmailAddress::fromMailbox()` accepts display-name syntax (`Eve <attacker@example.test>`), while the Foundation verification manager only treats a *bare* `FILTER_VALIDATE_EMAIL` identifier as the authoritative account email. The notifier previously preferred a display-name mailbox in an account's username over its legitimate `metadata.email`, potentially misrouting account-linked security notifications. Align the notifier's `storedAddress()` rule with the manager: accept only bare validated email identifiers, otherwise resolve an explicitly validated metadata email. Add a real fake-mailer regression proving a display-name username never shadows `metadata.email` for password reset or direct verification notifications. Concurrently record three previously reported strict Psalm exception contracts in the adjacent `AuthNotificationRegistrar` (without suppressions or static analysis configuration changes). Count **38/90** remains until exact-head verification.

- **P0 A01 queued-notification address-change regression (2026-11-05; exact-head QA pending):** Beyond direct fake-notifier routing, exercise a real username-account `AuthServices::emailVerification()->issue()` followed by an authoritative stored-address change *before* the queued notification reaches the real TalkingBytes fake `Emailer`. Verify the outbound adapter rejects the old queued token without delivery, the old token is invalid at redemption, a fresh request for the new stored address delivers only to that recipient, and the new token verifies the correct account. Assert notification account and purpose metadata. This closes the issuance/delivery TOCTOU test gap without inventing an unsupported email-change workflow or bypassing the current-account-only contract. Keep A01's remaining acceptance unchecked until exact-head QA; **38/90 accepted, 52 open**.

- **P0 A01 notifier delivery revalidation (2026-11-05; exact-head QA pending):** `EmailVerificationManager::issue()` and token redemption already enforce the account-stored recipient, but the final TalkingBytes adapter still allowed a direct `EMAIL_VERIFICATION_REQUESTED` notification to specify any syntactically valid `payload.email`. At the send boundary, require an existing account and match the explicit email against its authoritative identifier/metadata email, failing closed for a changed or unknown address. Extend `Foundation4SecurityBoundariesTest` to assert real `Emailer::fake()` delivery only to the stored address, and reject forged/missing-account direct notifications. No new email-change workflow or silent fallback; direct domain helpers stay purpose-specific, and privileged address changes require a separate authenticated contract. **P0 A01 2 remaining criteria and total acceptance 38/90 stay open until official PHPForge verification.**

- **P2 published Runwire 3 API and scope-shutdown accepted (2026-11-05; four-lane QA verified):** Exact source `605f88f4` passed four PHPForge functional QA configurations, two benchmarks, four production consumers, clean install and PHPStan ([41965242094](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41965242094)); strict Psalm still fails with **1,348 errors** per PHP version. `RunwireSelectedNativeBoundaryTest` pins public method names, named arguments, driver enums and cancellation. `RunwireCoroutineScopeIntegrationTest` proves scope-owned shutdown executes with an active root task, after once-only Foundation cleanup, with a still-usable borrowed host scope. These prove two P2 criteria only, not full host deployment, worker drain/fork/generation or sustained paired-host performance. All four real Swoole/OpenSwoole serving jobs passed in [41965241298](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41965241298); native-host completion is tracked separately. **Formal acceptance advances from 36/90 to 38/90, 52 open, P2 6/8; P0 remains 18/20** with destination policy and notifier matrix still open.

- **P2 Runwire scope shutdown acceptance evidence (2026-11-05; official QA pending):** On published Runwire 3.0, `CoroutineScope::execute()` closes owned resources in `finally` before `FiberScheduler::drive()` returns; `CoroutineScope::registerShutdown()` runs owned cleanup while the root task is active. Extend the existing Foundation/Runwire live-scheduler regression to register a real scope-owned shutdown callback, assert scheduler `activeTaskCount() > 0` inside it, and enforce the order of Foundation deferred cleanup, preserved primary exception, borrowed host access and final Runwire scope shutdown. A completed official [Security & Standards 41877200960](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41877200960) verified the pre-extension Runwire test on all four QA lanes, and [native-host 41877200338](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41877200338) passed. - **P0 A01 selected-backend account email verification (2026-11-05; official QA pending):** Source snapshot `dd0d0de3` completed [Security & Standards 41877200960](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41877200960) with four green functional QA lanes, two benchmarks, four production consumers, clean installation, and PHPStan in both analysis lanes; [native-host 41877200338](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41877200338) passed all five relevant jobs. - **P0 A01 DBLayer rollback exception preservation (2026-11-04; official QA pending):** The real DBLayer 6.1.4 transaction wrapper converts ordinary callback exceptions to `TransactionException::failed()` after rollback. The first in-memory interleaving regression established the invalid destination result, but a DBLayer-backed verification could surface the transaction wrapper rather than the same result. Add an internal `EmailDestinationChanged` signal and unwrap only the exact DBLayer successfully-rolled-back failure identity in `DBLayerEmailVerificationStore::consumeAndApply()`. Preserve transaction/rollback errors of other kinds, including rollback failure; keep `EmailVerificationManager` unaware of the optional DBLayer dependency. Add a real SQLite regression for unconsumed request after rejected callback, unrelated transaction exception preservation, and nullable account JSON path; resolve the remaining newly reported Psalm throw annotation. **P0 A01 acceptance still pending exact-head QA and live MySQL/PostgreSQL checks; overall 36/90, P0 18/20.**

- **P0 A01 database portability + affected Psalm contracts (2026-11-04; official QA pending):** The `e6f297d5` conditional write and interleaving/real SQLite regression passed all four functional PHPForge QA configurations in [41875190938](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41875190938); PHPStan passed, but Psalm increased from 1,373 to 1,377 due four added exception annotations. DBLayer 6.1.4 schema compilation confirms PostgreSQL `json()` is actual `JSON`, not `JSONB`; direct `metadata = ?` is not portable for PostgreSQL. Compare captured JSON bytes through explicit dialect SQL: PostgreSQL casts to TEXT, MySQL/MariaDB cast to BINARY, SQL Server compares converted VARBINARY, SQLite retains its binary-collated TEXT equality. Unsupported drivers fail explicitly. Add real SQLite direct-email / null-metadata coverage, document the new major-version storage contract, and add missing propagated-exception PHPDocs to the modified account-store operations without suppressions or behavior changes. Keep P0 A01 open pending selected-backend runtime checks and exact-head QA; **formal 36/90, P0 18/20.**

- **P0 A01 email-verification mutation-boundary batch (2026-11-04; official QA pending):** The prior domain guard checked the account email before the one-time request transaction, while the final account update still targeted only `id`. A concurrent address change could therefore invalidate the read before the write. Introduce an explicit `AccountStoreInterface::markVerifiedForEmail()` contract: in-memory stores compare the authoritative email before updating; DBLayer stores optimistically compare the original `identifier`, `status`, and raw `metadata` row at the SQL update boundary. `EmailVerificationManager` performs the conditional update inside `consumeAndApply()`, aborting the transaction/undoing in-memory token consumption on a mismatch and returning `email_destination_untrusted` without issuing an `EMAIL_VERIFIED` audit event. Keep administrative `markVerified()` separate. Add deterministic interleaving and real SQLite conditional-update regressions; live MySQL/PostgreSQL JSON behavior and inter-process contention still need selected-backend verification. **Do not accept P0 A01 until exact-head official QA; 36/90 accepted, 54 open.**

- **P2 Runwire live-scheduler QA correction (2026-11-04; exact-head QA pending):** Official exact-head [Security & Standards 41870090217](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41870090217) for `b74e5d9d` completed with 920 passed / 1 failed Pest assertions in all four QA lanes; PHPStan, Pint, PHPCS, Rector, Deptrac, benchmarks, production consumers and clean install passed. [Native host 41870088724](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41870088724) passed including Swoole/OpenSwoole. The lone Pest failure at `RunwireCoroutineScopeIntegrationTest:266` arises because an arrow wrapper captures the outside `$events` array by value even though the inner callback captures its local binding by reference. Replace that wrapper with an explicit by-reference capture, retaining the live-host-scope, reverse cleanup and primary-exception assertions. Confirmed PHP arrow capture semantics with PHP 8.4 locally. Psalm remains **1,373 errors** per 8.4/8.5 lane; no suppression, quality-gate relaxation or production control-flow change. **Formal acceptance remains 36/90 (P0 18/20), 54 open** until official exact-head verification. Independently audit the email-verification account destination recheck versus `markVerified` mutation for a possible concurrent account-email change; no confirmed exploitable race or acceptance claim until a deterministic regression.

Work is underway on PR #20 / `feature/foundation-3`; no merge, tag, publication, or Infbyte source changes have been made. The pre-implementation audit and version matrix below remain historical baseline evidence, not assertions about the current working branch.

- **P0 partial:** hardened account-authoritative password-reset delivery, recursively sanitized authentication-audit metadata, made route presets fail closed, bound trusted config/worker artifacts to captured verified bytes, and supervised process stdin through the timeout/output pump. Added focused regression tests. Follow-up coverage is still needed for end-to-end notification delivery, adversarial artifact swapping, independent audit sinks, and process cancellation. A07 now has project-specific Deptrac classification and a negative app-dependency regression, verified in four lanes; broader graph review remains.
- **P1 partial:** raised `composer.json` Core/optional release floors, added optional-package exclusion bounds, synchronized both module catalogs, production-consumer installation manifests, and selected fixture expectations. Runwire 3 replaces Runwire 2 without dual-version support. Published dependency resolution and consumer checks are validated through hosted CI, but all other migration gates require final-source verification.
- **P2–P3 in progress:** preserved ordinary PHP and host-borrowed Runwire paths; OTP 7.0.1 challenge/verification/passkey operations now borrow the current context. Epicrypt file protection and Pathwise HTTP upload/chunk/finalization/download preparation pass operation-local context. Added cancellation and state-preservation regressions. Broader specialist/live-service qualification remains open.
- **P4–P5 partial:** synchronized version labels in the migration/release/OTP/authentication/filesystem/operations documentation and native API test descriptions; documented operation-local Runwire forwarding, ordinary PHP fallback and response-stream ownership. Exhaustive documentation and compatibility review remain open.
- **P6 open:** the pre-batch source `27ded8d61fd9bb20b3561cb5b7f6af04eb95ad62` passed all four stable/lowest PHP 8.4/8.5 QA and production-consumer lanes, both static-analysis and benchmark lanes, and release evidence in [Security & Standards 41652684379](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41652684379). [Native host 41652683685](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41652683685) passed Swoole/OpenSwoole PHP 8.4/8.5 and ordinary smoke; paired-host jobs were skipped because they require explicit workflow dispatch. New edits need their own exact-source QA. Strict source-only and whole-dependency-upgrade paired-host throughput, retained-state rollback, specialist/live-backend qualification, and final release evidence remain blockers.
- **Additional security improvement:** removed untrusted notification payload from TalkingBytes email message metadata, preventing reset tokens and caller context from leaking into transport-level metadata while preserving rendered message content. The privileged PHPForge release workflow is pinned to an immutable revision.
- **Latest verified rolling implementation:** ReqShield upload/MIME/path and `FormRequest` cancellation regressions (`5e6e5ba7`), account-authoritative passwordless/MFA/security notification recipients (`da1aa9b7`, `2828e444`), 120-second host comparator (`2790750c`), audited credential persistence, captured release artifact loads and bounded process cancellation are included in the four-lane green pre-batch CI. These do not certify full real backends or paired-host performance.

The 90 implementation checkboxes below are acceptance criteria. Mark only entries with implementation and focused test/CI evidence; unmarked entries remain release requirements, even when partially implemented.

- **Verified release-gate checkpoint (2026-11-04):** `0902665b` passed all four QA PHP 8.4/8.5 stable/lowest lanes, both static-analysis lanes (including required Psalm), both benchmark lanes, four production consumers and release-evidence in [41761279913](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41761279913). Both native PHP 8.4/8.5 Swoole and OpenSwoole lanes and the host smoke passed [41761279328](https://github.com/infocyph/Foundation/actions/runs/41761279328). These are functional tests, not the missing controlled paired-host or multi-service recovery certificates. Keep `infocyph/phpforge: dev-main@dev`; the separate immutable reusable Actions workflow ref selects only the workflow code, not a fixed Composer package release.
- **Analysis/tooling reconciliation (2026-11-04):** `453d92d` CI `41760817282` passed PHPStan and Composer audit but failed Psalm on PHP 8.4/8.5 solely because pinned PHPForge reusable workflow `fdec64c` calls `vendor/bin/psalm.phar` while current PHPForge `dev-main` resolves to `9b6067d` and installs the Composer binary `vendor/bin/psalm`. Pinning the reviewed published upstream workflow source at `9b6067dcf8a477d3420c178d934e4dbc6a589b49` restores the supported executable path. The new revision requires all QA and analysis lanes to pass; Psalm remains required.

## Objective and agreed boundaries

Upgrade Foundation 4 to the latest published library releases and integrate their current APIs throughout the framework. Audit the complete library for vulnerabilities, correctness defects, integration gaps and worthwhile improvements, and track required remediation here. Foundation is to Infbyte what Illuminate is to Laravel: Foundation supplies reusable framework components, composition and public integration contracts; Infbyte supplies the application experience built on them. Foundation must be independently installable, testable and usable by other applications.

The requirements for this work are:

- Latest published library versions form the new minimum supported baseline. Remove compatibility paths for older library versions/APIs; do not offer dual Runwire 2/3 support or silently select an older dependency to pass a check.
- Every applicable integration works with or without an active Runwire host. Ordinary PHP execution and execution with a borrowed Runwire context are supported modes. Synchronous operation without Runwire context is intentional behavior, not a legacy-version fallback.
- Support shared hosting, VPS, dedicated/in-house servers, containers and other environments meeting the declared PHP/platform requirements. Ordinary web hosting must not require a Foundation-managed listener, process supervisor, PCNTL, POSIX, Swoole, QUIC or a persistent worker. Feature-specific prerequisites remain explicit.
- Runwire remains a required Core Composer dependency in this Foundation 4 design. Installing its package does not activate a runtime. Optional Runwire integration in the specialist packages must continue working when those packages are used independently without Runwire installed.
- A supplied stale, mismatched, cancelled or expired context must not be treated as an absent context and rerun synchronously. Unsupported explicitly selected drivers/features must fail clearly.
- Foundation owns application composition, capability policy, release generations and application execution coordination. Webrick owns HTTP adaptation/request scope, InterMix owns DI scopes, and the selected host/Runwire owns its listeners, process model, event loop and native runtime lifecycle. Specialist libraries retain their mechanisms.
- Keep optional specialists optional. Package installation does not activate a feature. Maintain the three module groups, eight selectable features and current `schema_version: 2` module contract.
- Work in this branch as Foundation 4 regardless of its branch name. Infbyte source changes and application publication are separate downstream work.

Follow the installed PHPForge [engineering principles](../vendor/infocyph/phpforge/resources/engineering-principles.md) and [agent workflow](../vendor/infocyph/phpforge/resources/AGENTS.md). Preserve correctness, security, lifecycle ownership and the existing performance budgets. Do not edit `vendor/`.

## Historical evidence collected for the original plan

The inspected Foundation source is `cbaaa59ff9b60d0cf9cb13ba4a784fe859178409`; the worktree was clean before planning. Existing Foundation 4 architecture, generated-runtime implementation, module catalogs, integration tests, migration documentation and host workflows were inspected. The existing Graphify graph was used for navigation; source is authoritative.

Live Composer/Packagist queries on the date above established the version matrix below. `composer show --direct --latest --format=json` reports the local dependency set at the latest available direct releases except Runwire. `composer outdated --direct --format=json` reports Runwire `2.1.2 -> 3.0`. Installed versions do not establish that Foundation's declared lower bounds or integrations are correct.

The local `composer.lock` is ignored by Git. Its SHA-256 at inspection was `8fdf346ad7ab148e1c0a5af74b8cec15d5ab0cbd6a6482e779a5fad82ef59d7e`; the manifest SHA-256 was `5d01d8ac1f5d8442835cba3e860e7a44aebf883c859658dd7bf9ad7f860970da`. Preserve library lockfile policy; retain resolved locks as validation artifacts. Infbyte/application deployments own their committed application lockfiles.

The proposed ranges and optional-package conflicts were resolved in a temporary directory using a copy of the manifest/lock:

```sh
composer update --with-all-dependencies --dry-run --no-install \
    --no-scripts --no-plugins --no-interaction --prefer-stable
```

Result: zero installs/removals, one update (`infocyph/runwire 2.1.2 -> 3.0`), and no security vulnerability advisories reported. Composer reported the abandoned development transitive dependency `doctrine/annotations`. This is resolution evidence only; it does not certify API compatibility, runtime behavior or production consumers. Foundation's manifest, lock and installed dependencies were not changed.

`composer ic:doctor`, `composer ic:list-config` and `composer ic:active-config` completed; doctor reported a healthy setup. The subsequent baseline audit, test failures and production-consumer results are recorded below. The planned Runwire 3 dependency change has not been applied or certified.
